# ZeroProxy Implementation Status

Date: 2026-06-09

This document is the implementation-status placeholder for the active
QuickJS/GoNetworkBackend migration. The detailed technical plan lives in
`PLAN.md`; this file tracks what has actually landed in the repository.

Old Service Worker/Rust-rewriter release-gate status has been intentionally
removed. Do not re-add stale status text. As implementation progresses, update
this document with completed work, verification results, known gaps, and any
intentional deviations from `PLAN.md`.

## Current Target

Planned architecture:

```text
QuickJS-NG WASM
  -> JS Web API stubs / virtual browser bridge
  -> GoNetworkBackend
     -> WorkerBackend: Dedicated Worker + wasm_exec.js + kernel.wasm
     -> ForegroundBackend: same Go WASM kernel fallback
  -> existing Go WASM network engine
  -> WebSocket/smux/SOCKS5/uTLS
```

Route policy:

- Keep the existing share URL format:
  `/zp/p/<encrypted>#k=<key>[&server=<relay>...]`.
- Do not introduce a replacement document-route namespace.
- Remove Service Worker interception from the new runtime path.
- Keep the Go WASM network engine.
- Remove Rust JS rewriter/codegen from the target-JS execution hot path.

## Current Code-State Audit (2026-06-09 continuation)

This audit was run before resuming implementation work, per the current task
instruction. It is based on current file contents and the verification commands
listed below, not on prior session memory.

Current assessment:

- The migration is still **not PLAN-complete**. The active repository state
  contains the QuickJS/GoNetworkBackend cutover and legacy Service Worker route
  deletion, but Web API/browser-behavior parity remains explicitly partial.

  entries: 219 implemented, 726 partial, 0 blocked by gap, 29 blocked by policy,
  5 out of scope, and 0 hidden missing entries. It also records both
  `globalThis` Set diffs: the pristine engine baseline has 979 host keys, 71
  QuickJS keys, 911 browser-only-before-install keys, 68 shared QuickJS-native
  keys, and 3 QuickJS-only keys; the installed virtual-browser snapshot has
  1060 QuickJS keys, 977 shared host-browser keys, 2 browser-only-after-install

  queued chunk behavior, `StylePropertyMapReadOnly` uses the browser-shaped
  `entries` iterator function, and CSSOM rule/sheet mutator arity now matches
  Chromium while preserving no-argument defaults.
  `MediaSource.setLiveSeekableRange()` records and clears live seekable state
  instead of being a no-op; full media-element seekable integration remains
  partial.
- `clientInformation` uses a browser-shaped global accessor descriptor aliasing
  `navigator`; its returned Navigator object still exposes virtual own-key
  state and therefore remains in `entryShapeDiffs` as a partial surface.
- `Node` now exposes the legacy entity constants plus namespace/baseURI/nodeValue
  descriptor family, but remains mismatched because `ChildNode`/non-standard text
  helpers still live on `Node.prototype` and its `@@toStringTag` is intentionally
  not set until element/SVG tag behavior can be moved safely.
- `URLPattern` host-bridge reflection is now browser-host-only; Node's non-browser
  descriptor snapshot no longer rewrites QuickJS URLPattern accessors to the
  wrong configurability during surface tests.
- Policy-virtualized entries whose host browser snapshot currently lacks a
  comparable constructor (`DeviceMotionEvent`, `DeviceOrientationEvent`,
  `LockManager`, `Lock`, `KeyboardLayoutMap`, `WakeLock`,
  `WakeLockSentinel`, and `Credential`) now carry explicit
  `expectedShapeDeltas: ["host-missing"]`; they remain covered by behavior
  probes but no longer inflate implemented descriptor mismatches.
- Active source inspection currently finds no `/zp/api/fetch`,
  `/zp/api/script`, `/zp/api/worker-script`, `/zp/sw.js`,
  `serviceWorker.register('/zp/sw.js')`, active `ZPRewriter`/
  `ZPHTTPRewriter`, or browser-packaged Rust rewriter asset references in
  `web`, `cmd`, `internal`, `scripts`, `rewriter-rs/src`, or active tests,
  except tests/fixtures that assert those names remain absent or fail closed.
- `web/sw.js`, `web/sw-entry.mjs`, `web/sw/`, `web/http-rewriter.js`,
  `web/rust-rewriter.js`, and `web/js-rewriter.js` are absent in the current
  working tree.
- `scripts/build.mjs` still emits `runtime-prelude.js` and
  `worker-prelude.js`; these are current legacy/containment facade assets, not
  proof of full target-browser API parity. `web/runtime-prelude.mjs` still
  contains `scriptProxyPath`/`resourceProxyPath` references for fail-closed and
  native-membrane containment code. The active host-shell path remains
  `web/host-shell-entry.mjs` -> `VirtualResourceLoader` -> QuickJS realm
  evaluation through `QuickJSRealm.evalClassic`/`evalModule`.
- Current blockers before `PLAN.md` completion remain the partial surfaces
  enumerated by the Web API gap report: Window/property descriptors,
  element-specific property/descriptors beyond constructor branding,
  parser/tree-construction edge cases, XML/namespace semantics, Range/Traversal
  mutation and remaining geometry/descriptors, Selection modify/composed-range
  algorithm edge parity,
  MutationRecord payload edge parity, form submission/validation algorithms
  beyond covered submitter/fieldset/external-owner/dirname/formdata-event handling,
  contexts, CSS cascade/layout computed values, WebSocket failure/backpressure
  behavior, FileReader encoding/timing edge parity, FormData stream/object-URL/
  multipart parity, redirect/credential edge semantics, ReadableStream bodies,
  cookie/browser storage edge behavior,
  IndexedDB indexes/cursors, Cache API eviction, history traversal

## WebIDL Host-Bridge Re-evaluation (2026-06-08 continuation)

User direction added during this continuation: most browser APIs should come
from host-browser WebIDL implementations through a membrane-safe bridge, not
from hand-written approximations. Direct QuickJS code remains appropriate only
where native host behavior would expose host DOM objects, host-origin storage,
native network fetches, privileged shell state, or wrong target-visible virtual
state.

Plan/status changes made:

- `PLAN.md` now has a `WebIDL Host-Bridge Policy` section: default strategy is
  `host-bridge`; unavoidable virtualized boundaries include
  `Window`/`WindowProxy`, `window.location`, `history`, virtual `Document`/DOM
  identity, event-loop scheduling, navigation, storage/cookies/cache, network
  APIs, workers, resource loading, policy-blocked APIs, and realm/lifetime glue.
- `PLAN.md` now requires `bridge-surface.json` entries to record an
  implementation strategy: `host-bridge`, `virtualized`, `direct-shim`, or
  `blocked`, plus a bridge-replacement decision for existing direct code.
- `PLAN.md` now explicitly forbids reimplementing, shadowing, or replacing
  QuickJS-NG's ECMAScript built-ins (`String`, `BigInt`, collections,
  typed-array constructors, promises, JSON, reflection, etc.) unless a
  narrowly documented membrane hook proves otherwise.
- `PLAN.md` now requires the implementation plan to consult the complete
  Set-level diff between host-browser `globalThis` and pristine QuickJS-NG
  `globalThis`; `browserOnly` entries feed `bridge-surface.json`,
  `quickJSNative` is the do-not-touch baseline for engine built-ins, and
  `quickJSOnly` catches accidental QuickJS-specific leaks.
- `PLAN.md` now forbids regex/text-scraping parsers for HTML/CSS/JS/WebIDL/API
  surfaces; surface extraction must use runtime APIs or maintained parsers such
  as host browser APIs, QuickJS-NG parsing, DOM/CSSOM APIs, `DOMParser`,
  `CSSStyleSheet`, `golang.org/x/net/html`, or an equivalent purpose-built
  parser.
- `web/runtime/webapi/core.mjs` no longer carries the large QuickJS injection as
  an inline `String.raw` blob. The realm source now lives in
  `web/runtime/webapi/core-realm-entry.mjs`, is bundled by
  `scripts/build-webapi-core.mjs`, and is imported from the generated
  `web/runtime/webapi/core-source.generated.mjs`. `scripts/build.mjs` runs this
  generator before building browser assets, and `package.json` exposes
  `npm run build:webapi-core`.
- `scripts/snapshot-browser-surface.mjs` now snapshots host
  `Reflect.ownKeys(globalThis)` through Puppeteer.
- `scripts/snapshot-quickjs-surface.mjs` now snapshots a pristine QuickJS-NG
  realm through the compiled runtime.
- `scripts/compare-webapi-surface.mjs` now emits the Set diff in both JSON and
  Markdown gap reports.
- Web API core URL resolution/reflection now goes through host-browser `URL`
  parsing via `__zpParseURL`/`__zpSetURLPart` host functions instead of the
  previous regex URL splitter; the QuickJS realm still receives only string/record
  data, not host DOM objects.
- Web API core element/form/storage helpers and the MutationObserver matcher were
  decomposed to satisfy Biome cognitive-complexity gates without changing their
  public behavior; `core-source.generated.mjs` was regenerated from
  `core-realm-entry.mjs`.
- QuickJS `DOMParser`, `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, and `document.write` no longer
  use the old hand-written regex/token scanner. `web/runtime/webapi/core.mjs`
  now exposes host `DOMParser` document snapshots and context-aware host
  fragment snapshots through `__zpHostParseHTMLDocument` and
  `__zpHostParseHTMLFragment`; the QuickJS realm rebuilds virtual nodes from
  plain records so no host DOM object crosses the membrane.
- `bridge-surface.json` now records `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `DOMParser`, and
  `document.write` as host-parser bridge users and keeps `XMLSerializer`
  virtualized because it serializes QuickJS virtual DOM nodes.
- `CSSStyleDeclaration.cssText` now delegates declaration parsing to the host CSS
  parser via `__zpHostParseStyleDeclarations`; QuickJS keeps declaration state,
  mutation, and computed-style snapshots virtualized.
- IndexedDB now covers basic index, key-range, cursor-direction, compound-key,
  and transaction-event APIs on the virtual durable substrate:
  `IDBObjectStore.createIndex/deleteIndex/index/openCursor`,
  `IDBIndex.get/getAll/openCursor`, `IDBKeyRange.only/lowerBound/upperBound/bound`,
  `IDBCursorWithValue.continue`, next/prev/unique cursor directions, compound
  array key-path indexes, transaction complete/error/abort handler basics,
  persisted index metadata, key-range filtering, and multiEntry/unique index
  validation.
- `navigator.storage` now exposes a virtual `StorageManager` facade with
  `estimate()`, `persisted()`, and `persist()`; `estimate()` reports current
  QuickJS-visible local/session storage, IndexedDB, and Cache API usage against
  the configured virtual partition quota, `persist()` records a virtual grant,
  and local/session storage, IndexedDB object-store writes, plus Cache API writes
  rollback with `QuotaExceededError` when exceeding that quota.

First-pass bridge replacement assessment for already-landed surfaces:

- Keep virtualized/direct for membrane-critical state: virtual
  `Window`/`Document`/`Node`/`Element` identity, `window.location`, `history`,
  event dispatch/listener storage, navigation, target network APIs
  (`fetch`/XHR/EventSource/WebSocket), `document.cookie`, virtual storage,
  HTTP cache/Cache API, workers, resource loading, and
  `navigator.serviceWorker` policy facade.
- Do not replace QuickJS-NG's own ECMAScript built-ins. `String`, `BigInt`,
  arrays, typed arrays, collection constructors, promises, JSON, and reflection
  remain engine-owned even when the same names are present in the host browser's
  `globalThis`.
- Prefer host-bridge replacement/reuse for safe WebIDL algorithms and
  descriptors: `structuredClone`, `atob`/`btoa`, Blob/File/FormData algorithms,
  DOMParser/XMLSerializer/HTML/XML parsing and serialization, URL/URLSearchParams
  parsing, CSSStyleDeclaration/CSS parsing/computed-style descriptor surfaces,
  collection descriptor/brand details, Range/Selection/Traversal edge
  algorithms, and Navigator/Permissions descriptor shape where values remain
  policy-controlled.
- Keep direct-shim only with explicit justification: synchronous
  `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` mirrors preloaded from the virtual storage
  manager, policy-safe navigator identity/device/plugin arrays, permissions and
  clipboard stubs, and any host API whose native side effects cannot be
  suppressed.
- Next implementation work should update `bridge-surface.json` with the new
  strategy/decision fields before broadening API coverage, then migrate the
  bridge-safe direct shims above toward host bridges one family at a time with
  behavior probes.

## Current Conservative Audit (2026-06-08)

This audit was refreshed after the Phase 10 AST-rewriter hot-path cleanup. It
combines static source review with the verification results recorded below.

Repository-backed findings:

- Implemented and source-backed:
  - Service Worker source assets and `/zp/api/*` route generation are absent
    from active source (`web/`, `cmd/`, `internal/`, `scripts/`, `rewriter-rs`);
    the only matches are deletion/absence tests.
  - `web/runtime/resources/loader.mjs` fetches documents/resources through
    `GoNetworkBackend`, calls sanitizer RPCs, and evaluates inline/external
    target script source with `QuickJSRealm.evalClassic`/`evalModule`.
  - `scripts/build.mjs` builds QuickJS-NG from `third_party/quickjs-ng` source
    with `emcc` and publishes `quickjs-runtime.mjs`/`quickjs-runtime.wasm`.
  - Active browser build/server/runtime source no longer packages or calls
    `ZPRewriter`, `ZPHTTPRewriter`, `virtual:zeroproxy-rust-rewriter`,
    `web/http-rewriter.js`, `rust-rewriter.js`, or `rust-rewriter.wasm`.
  - `rewriter-rs/Cargo.toml` and compiled Rust source no longer include
    SWC Ecma parser/codegen dependencies or modules; `rewrite_script` and
    `rewrite_script_with_context` now return
    `UNSUPPORTED_TARGET_JS_REWRITE`.
  - `cmd/wasm-kernel/main.go` no longer delegates document rewrite to a JS
    global; non-raw legacy HTML document transform now fails closed with
    `HTML_DOCUMENT_TRANSFORM_UNAVAILABLE`, while raw document mode remains the
    sanitizer input path.
  - `internal/cssrewrite/rewrite.go` uses
    `github.com/tdewolff/parse/v2/css` for Go-side CSS URL collection/rewrite.
  - `web/runtime/webapi/storage.mjs` contains the current durable-storage
    substrate and partitioned local/session/IndexedDB/Cache API/HTTP-cache
    mirrors.
- Remaining gaps:
  - Browser API parity is not complete: the checked-in Web API gap report has
    979 tracked entries, with 219 implemented, 726 partial, 29 blocked by policy,
    5 out of scope, and 0 hidden missing entries; after installing the virtual
    event-loop, DOM, network, and WebAPI facades only `Temporal` and
    `WebAssembly` remain browser globals absent from QuickJS, and both are
    runtime-compatibility out of scope. The descriptor/prototype shape audit
    covers 930 global entries and reports 426 mismatching shape entries; those
    remaining mismatches are partial entries or explicit expected host-missing
    deltas for policy-virtualized surfaces, not overclaimed implemented parity.
  - Partial browser-behavior surfaces still include parser fidelity for
    element-specific properties/descriptors and HTML/SVG tree-construction edge cases,
    SVG animated-value/list geometry APIs, form submission and remaining validation algorithms,
    resource loading/error lifecycles, Range/traversal mutation, remaining geometry,
    and long-tail event-constructor edge cases,
    failure-timing/transport-backpressure edge cases, CSS cascade/inheritance
    and layout computed values, `ReadableStream` request/response body
    surfaces, redirect policy and cross-origin credential edge cases,
    cookie/storage browser edge semantics, IndexedDB
    indexes/cursors, Cache API eviction, host permission prompt/state-change
    integration, full navigation side effects, and additional device/media
    capability details.
  - The Rust helper crate still exists outside the active browser build for
    helper/characterization surfaces. Any future reuse must preserve the PLAN
    boundary: no target-JS AST/codegen hot path in the browser runtime.

Conservative status: the Phase 10 Rust/AST target-JS hot-path exclusion is now
source- and test-backed for the active path, but the migration is not
PLAN-complete until the remaining real-browser Web API and behavior parity gaps
are closed or explicitly reclassified.

## Phase 0 Inventory Snapshot (pre-shell decoupling)

- Service Worker boot/control at Phase 0:
  - Before Phase 2, `web/index.html` registered `/zp/sw.js`, waited for
    `navigator.serviceWorker.ready`, and required
    `navigator.serviceWorker.controller` before opening share routes.
  - `web/sw.js` classifies native requests, owns `install`/`activate`/`fetch`/
    `message` handlers, manages tab/share-route state, and exposes runtime RPCs:
    `ZP_STATUS`, `ZP_ENSURE_READY`, `ZP_OPEN_SHARE`, `ZP_FRAME_ROUTE`,
    `ZP_HISTORY_UPDATE`, `ZP_BASE_UPDATE`, `ZP_RESOLVE_ENTRY`,
    `ZP_SCROLL_UPDATE`, `ZP_COOKIE_SET`, `ZP_FETCH_ABORT`,
    `ZP_UPLOAD_STREAM_OPEN`, `ZP_TRANSPORT_TIMINGS`, and `ZP_WS_OPEN`.
- `/zp/api/*` routes:
  - `web/sw/routes.js` recognizes only `/zp/api/fetch`, `/zp/api/script`, and
    `/zp/api/worker-script` as runtime API routes.
  - At Phase 0, `web/runtime/network/http.mjs`, `web/runtime-prelude.mjs`,
    `web/runtime/workers/facades.mjs`, and `web/worker-prelude.js` built
    those routes for target fetch/script/worker execution.
  - At Phase 0, `cmd/zeroproxy-server/main.go` served shell assets, share
    routes, `/zp/kernel.wasm`, `/zp/ws-pipe`, and worker bootstrap assets;
    `/zp/api/*` depended on Service Worker interception.
- Go WASM bridge:
  - `cmd/wasm-kernel/main.go` exports `__go_jshttp`, `__zp_stream`,
    `__zp_kernel_init`, `__zp_cookie_set`, and `__zp_kernel_ready`.
  - `web/sw/kernel.js` loads `/zp/kernel.wasm` and calls `__zp_kernel_init`.
  - `web/sw.js` calls `__go_jshttp` for HTTP, `__zp_cookie_set` for document
    cookies, and `__zp_stream` for target WebSocket streams.
  - `internal/swhttp/bridge_js_test.go`, `cmd/wasm-kernel/deliver_test.go`,
    `cmd/wasm-kernel/wsstream_test.go`, and
    `cmd/wasm-kernel/bridge_characterization_test.go` are the current bridge
    characterization set.
- Rust rewriter hot path:
  - `rewriter-rs/src/js/swc_rewriter.rs` performs whole-program JS codegen.
  - At Phase 0, `rewriter-rs/src/js/module_urls.rs`,
    `rewriter-rs/src/import_map/*`, and `rewriter-rs/src/html/*` emitted
    `/zp/api/script` and `/zp/api/fetch` URLs for native execution.
  - At Phase 0, `web/http-rewriter.js`, `scripts/build.mjs`,
    `web/runtime-prelude.mjs`, and `web/worker-prelude.js` packaged/used the
    Rust rewriter on the legacy hot path.

## Phase 1 GoNetworkBackend Snapshot

- Protocol and records:
  - `web/runtime/network/protocol.mjs` now owns the versioned message envelope
    (`v`, `type`, `id`, `tabId`, optional `parentId`) and hello/ready feature
    negotiation.
  - It defines fetch, WebSocket, cookie, stream-control, cache, storage,
    health, shutdown, error, and cancellation message constructors.
  - Transferable streams are recognized as an optional negotiated feature; the
    required fallback remains chunked transferable `ArrayBuffer` messages.
- Backend adapters:
  - `web/runtime/network/gonetworkbackend.mjs` exports `GoNetworkBackend`,
    `WorkerBackend`, `ForegroundBackend`, `DispatcherBackend`, and
    `PostMessageRPC`.
  - `GoNetworkBackend.init()` prefers WorkerBackend and falls back to
    ForegroundBackend unless fallback is explicitly disabled.
  - ForegroundBackend uses the same dispatcher contract and passes an
    `AbortSignal` to kernel fetch calls; no native target `fetch` fallback is
    used.
- Dedicated network worker:
  - `web/network-worker.js` is a classic worker asset that loads
    `/zp/assets/wasm_exec.js`, instantiates `/zp/kernel.wasm`, calls
    `__zp_kernel_init`, and exposes protocol RPC over `postMessage`.
  - Response bodies are emitted as ordered `fetch.response.chunk` records and
    `fetch.response.end`; cancellation aborts the in-flight request signal.
  - `scripts/build.mjs`, `cmd/zeroproxy-server/main.go`, and
    `cmd/zeroproxy-server/routing_test.go` now include `network-worker.js` as a
    normal static asset.
- Tests:
  - `test/js/gonetworkbackend.test.js` covers envelopes, negotiation, all Phase
    1 message families, chunking, credit/pause/resume/cancel, foreground kernel
    dispatch without native target fetch, WorkerBackend boot URL, and worker to
    foreground fallback.

## Phase 2 Service Worker Removal Shell Snapshot

- Shell boot:
  - `web/index.html` now loads `/zp/assets/host-shell.js` after
    `/zp/assets/zp-core.js`; it contains no `navigator.serviceWorker`
    registration, controller wait, `ZP_ENSURE_READY`, or `ZP_OPEN_SHARE`
    dependency.
  - `web/host-shell-entry.mjs` handles control-scope routing, share URL
    encryption/decryption, relay fragment parsing, and backend startup.
- Backend selection:
  - Host shell initializes `GoNetworkBackend` with
    `/zp/assets/network-worker.js` and keeps a foreground fallback path that
    loads `/zp/assets/wasm_exec.js` plus `/zp/kernel.wasm`.
  - `?zp_backend=foreground` forces ForegroundBackend for parity/smoke checks.
  - `window.__ZP_NETWORK_BACKEND`, `window.__ZP_NETWORK_BACKEND_READY`, and
    `window.__ZP_CURRENT_SHARE` expose host-side state for the next virtual
    renderer phase.
- Static routing:
  - `scripts/build.mjs` now emits `host-shell.js`.
  - `cmd/zeroproxy-server/main.go` and
    `cmd/zeroproxy-server/routing_test.go` allow `/zp/assets/host-shell.js`
    and `/zp/assets/network-worker.js`.
  - `/zp/ws-pipe`, `/zp/kernel.wasm`, and share-route `/zp/p/` server routes
    remain available.
- Known gap:
  - Target document rendering is intentionally pending on the QuickJS/sanitizer
    phases; opening a share records `__ZP_CURRENT_SHARE` without fetching target
    content in the native renderer.

## Phase 3 QuickJS Build And Host Bridge Snapshot

- Source pin/build:
  - `.gitmodules` pins `third_party/quickjs-ng` to
    `https://github.com/quickjs-ng/quickjs.git` (current checkout
    `fd0a0210b7be00957751871e7e01b8291268fc29`).
  - `scripts/build.mjs` compiles upstream `quickjs.c`, `libregexp.c`,
    `libunicode.c`, `dtoa.c`, and ZeroProxy `quickjs-runtime/bridge.c` with
    `emcc`; npm QuickJS packages are not used.
  - Build output is `/zp/assets/quickjs-runtime.mjs` plus
    `/zp/assets/quickjs-runtime.wasm`, both server-allowlisted.
- Host ABI:
  - `quickjs-runtime/bridge.c` exports `zp_qjs_create_realm`,
    `zp_qjs_destroy_realm`, `zp_qjs_eval`, `zp_qjs_get_prop`,
    `zp_qjs_define_host_function`, `zp_qjs_drain_jobs`,
    `zp_qjs_release_handle`, and `zp_qjs_handle_refcount`.
  - `web/runtime/quickjs/engine.mjs` wraps the C ABI, decodes JSON envelopes,
    routes host function calls, preserves wrapper identity by handle, and
    enforces realm teardown.
  - `web/host-shell-entry.mjs` boots QuickJS during shell backend startup and
    records `window.__ZP_QUICKJS_READY`.
- CSS parser update requested by user:
  - `internal/cssrewrite` now uses `github.com/tdewolff/parse/v2/css` for Go
    stylesheet/style-attribute URL rewriting.
  - Legacy Rust CSS rewriting remains in place until the sanitizer/loader phase
    moves target rendering onto the Go sanitizer path.

## Phase 4 Virtual Event Loop And Navigation Snapshot

- Scheduler:
  - `web/runtime/quickjs/event-loop.mjs` owns virtual task and microtask queues
    for a QuickJS realm.
  - Timers and intervals are host functions that retain QuickJS callback
    handles until fire, clear, or navigation teardown.
  - `queueMicrotask` stores QuickJS callbacks in the virtual microtask queue;
    QuickJS promise jobs are drained at each microtask checkpoint.
- Navigation lifecycle:
  - Hard navigation cancels timers, releases retained handles, destroys the old
    realm, creates a new realm, and reinstalls host globals.
  - Same-document navigation preserves the existing realm while updating virtual
    location state.
  - Console error calls and uncaught callback failures are captured for host
    diagnostics instead of escaping into native target execution.

## Phase 5 Resource Sanitizer And Loader Snapshot

- Raw document fetch:
  - `GoNetworkBackend.fetchRaw()` still uses the existing Go WASM network
    engine, but raw-mode requests now skip legacy HTML rewriting in
    `cmd/wasm-kernel/main.go`.
  - `internal/swhttp.ResponseToJSWithPolicy` preserves target headers for
    raw-mode sanitizer input, including HTTP `Link`.
- Sanitizer:
  - `internal/htmlsanitize` uses `golang.org/x/net/html` for document parsing
    and emits schema records such as `node.create`, `node.attr`,
    `resource.discovered`, `resource.blocked`, `script.inline`,
    `script.external`, `module.external`, `style.inline`, `style.external`,
    `navigation.link`, `navigation.form`, `frame.virtualDocument`,
    `favicon.decision`, and `document.end`.
  - URL-bearing HTML attributes, `srcset`, inline styles, style elements, and
    stylesheet CSS URLs are deny-by-default and renderer-facing values become
    `zp-internal://resource/...`, `about:blank#zp-nav-*`, `about:blank#zp-frame-*`,
    or blob URLs after loader fetch.
  - Browser-initiated hints are neutralized for rel token hints and HTTP
    `Link` preload/preconnect/prefetch-like headers. Favicon defaults to block
    and also has a fetch mode covered by tests.
- Loader:
  - `web/runtime/resources/loader.mjs` fetches the target document through
    `GoNetworkBackend`, calls `sanitize.document`, fetches discovered resources
    through the same backend, calls `sanitize.stylesheet` for external CSS, and
    tracks/revokes per-document blob URLs.
  - Classic and module script records execute in QuickJS realms; native browser
    scripts are not created.
  - `web/host-shell-entry.mjs` resets the QuickJS realm on hard share-route
    navigation, loads the virtual document, and exposes
    `window.__ZP_VIRTUAL_DOCUMENT` for tests/diagnostics.

## Phase 6 Virtual DOM Core And Renderer Snapshot

- QuickJS DOM core:
  - `web/runtime/dom/virtual-dom.mjs` installs `Document`, `Element`, `Node`,
    text/comment nodes, attributes, namespace attributes, Window/HTMLElement
    branding, `querySelectorAll` and `childNodes` as branded non-Array
    NodeList live collections with prototype `item`/iteration helpers, branded
    HTMLCollection live collections with prototype `item`/`namedItem`, basic
    selector lookup, and mutation operations inside the QuickJS realm.
  - Renderer-facing mutations emit deterministic `dom.*` records through the
    host ABI; target JS receives only QuickJS virtual DOM objects.
- Events and renderer bridge:
  - `EventTarget` listener storage handles capture, target, bubble ordering,
    `once`, passive default-prevention behavior, reentrancy, IDL `on*`
    properties, and compiled inline handler attributes.
  - `NativeRenderer` renders sanitizer-created nodes into `#zp-render-root` and
    forwards native click/input/change/submit/key events into QuickJS virtual
    event dispatch.
- Viewport/layout:
  - Virtual viewport state exposes `innerWidth`, `innerHeight`,
    `devicePixelRatio`, window scroll offsets/methods, branded `screen`, branded
    stable `visualViewport`, `matchMedia`/branded `MediaQueryList`, branded
    `performance`, `performance.now`,
    `getBoundingClientRect`, `ResizeObserver`, `IntersectionObserver`,
    and `requestAnimationFrame` callbacks on the virtual event loop.
  - `web/host-shell-entry.mjs` installs the virtual DOM before target scripts
    execute, so visible DOM is rendered through the native backend while target
    scripts mutate the QuickJS DOM.

## Phase 7 Network API Stubs Snapshot

- QuickJS network APIs:
  - `web/runtime/network/api.mjs` installs `fetch`, `Request`, `Response`,
    `Headers`, `AbortController`/`AbortSignal`, `XMLHttpRequest`,
    `EventSource`, `WebSocket`, and `document.cookie` inside the QuickJS realm.
  - Host calls route through `GoNetworkBackend.fetchRaw`, `setCookie`,
    `cancel`, and `openWebSocket`; no native target `fetch` fallback is used.
  - `web/runtime/resources/loader.mjs` waits for virtual network idle after
    target script execution so async fetch/XHR/EventSource promises settle in
    QuickJS before loader diagnostics are summarized.
- Cache/timing:
  - `VirtualHTTPCache` is partitioned by virtual document origin and covers
    request cache modes (`default`, `reload`, `force-cache`, `only-if-cached`,
    `no-cache`, `no-store`), response `Cache-Control`, validators
    (`ETag`/`Last-Modified` → `If-None-Match`/`If-Modified-Since`), `304`
    revalidation, `Vary`, redirects/final URLs, and timing metadata.

## Phase 8 Web API Bridge Expansion Snapshot

- Web API core:
  - `web/runtime/webapi/core.mjs` installs browser host integration stubs for

    storage facades, history/location helpers, `navigator`, NavigatorUAData,
    `sendBeacon`, permissions/clipboard/serviceWorker policy stubs, parser-backed

    per-element HTML constructor branding, table section/row helpers, form-control/
    FileList/DataTransfer/FormData(form) selected-file and textarea/select basics, host-backed URL/URLSearchParams accessors and optional value overloads, anchor/resource URL reflection and element
    branding, Cache API facade,
    CSSStyleDeclaration/cssText/common aliases/basic computed-style
    normalization, branded DOMStringMap-backed dataset, DOMTokenList-backed classList, branded CharacterData
    plus constructible Text/Comment basics, style reflection, branded

  - Host shell installs the Web API core after the virtual DOM and network APIs,

- Surface tracking:
  - `test/fixtures/webapi/bridge-surface.json` records every implemented,

  - `scripts/snapshot-browser-surface.mjs`,
    `scripts/snapshot-quickjs-surface.mjs`,
    `scripts/compare-webapi-surface.mjs`, and
    `scripts/probe-webapi-behavior.mjs` generate the host/QuickJS snapshot
    contract, machine-readable gap report, markdown report, and behavior-probe

## Phase 8 Storage/Cache Manager Snapshot

- Durable substrate:
  - `web/runtime/webapi/storage.mjs` owns the
    `zeroproxy-virtual-browser` IndexedDB schema with `origins`, `tabs`,
    `cookies`, `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, `indexedDBCatalog`,
    `indexedDBObjectData`, `httpCacheEntries`, `httpCacheBodies`,
    `cacheApiMetadata`, `cacheApiBodies`, `blobRegistry`, `quotaUsage`, and
    `schemaMeta` stores.
  - Storage partitions include virtual origin, top-level tab/share ID,
    browsing context, and relay isolation key.
- QuickJS mirrors/facades:
  - `web/runtime/webapi/core.mjs` loads a synchronous local/session storage
    mirror before target scripts execute and persists writes back through the
    host storage manager.
  - The target `indexedDB` facade supports open/deleteDatabase, version upgrade,
    object stores, transactions, and get/put/add/delete/clear/getAll with
    storage-manager persistence.
  - The Cache API facade and `VirtualHTTPCache` persist cache metadata and
    chunked response bodies through the same manager while keeping Service
    Worker registration unsupported.
  - `web/host-shell-entry.mjs` preloads the storage snapshot before installing
    QuickJS network/Web API facades and wires the storage-backed HTTP cache into
    `installVirtualNetworkAPIs`.
- Diagnostics and tests:
  - Quota usage, cache decision explanation, schema-upgrade store creation, and
    storage-event dispatch are covered by `test/js/storage-manager.test.js`.
  - `test/js/webapi-core.test.js` covers preloaded mirrors, local/session
    writes, target IndexedDB persistence, and Cache API persistence.
  - `test/js/network-api.test.js` covers HTTP cache persistence/reload through
    the storage manager without a second backend fetch.
  - `web/runtime/network/api.mjs` preserves target `referrer` and
    `referrerPolicy` in `GoNetworkBackend` request records.

## Phase 9 Acid Compatibility Push Snapshot

- Acid-lite fixtures:
  - `test/fixtures/acid/acid-fixtures.json` defines the current compatibility
    target score across DOM Range/Traversal, CSSOM layout, SVG/XML, and
    event-loop ordering probes.
  - `scripts/acid-harness.mjs` writes `test/fixtures/acid/acid-report.json` and
    fails if score drops below target or failures are unmapped.
  - `test/js/acid-compat.test.js` runs the probes inside the QuickJS virtual
    browser and checks a deterministic visual serialization hash as the initial
    screenshot-comparison gate.
- Gap mapping:
  - Acid failures map back to `webapi-gap-report.json`; current acid-lite report
    has no failures and no `acidBlocking` Web API gaps.


## Phase 10 Native JS Rewriter Hot-Path Removal Snapshot

- Target script execution:
  - `web/runtime/resources/loader.mjs` fetches external script resources through
    `GoNetworkBackend` and evaluates inline/external source with
    `QuickJSRealm.evalClassic`/`evalModule`; native `<script src>` proxy
    generation is not on the active path.
  - `web/runtime-prelude.mjs` no longer builds `ZP.apiPath('script')` URLs and
    blocks dynamically inserted native script URLs with `POLICY_BLOCKED`.
  - `web/runtime/dynamic-code/facade.mjs` no longer calls Rust/native script
    rewrite helpers; dynamic native-code compilation paths fail closed with
    `NotSupportedError` when CSP would otherwise require a rewrite.
  - `cmd/wasm-kernel/main.go` no longer calls a JS `ZPRewriter` document bridge;
    non-raw legacy HTML document transform fails closed, and raw document mode
    remains the sanitizer path.
  - `web/runtime/network/http.mjs` now fails closed for non-internal native
    runtime fetches instead of constructing `/zp/api/fetch`.
- Rust/helper scope:
  - `scripts/build.mjs` now builds QuickJS-NG directly and does not emit
    `rust-rewriter.js`, `rust-rewriter.wasm`, or `http-rewriter.js`;
    `cmd/zeroproxy-server/main.go` does not serve those assets.
  - `rewriter-rs/Cargo.toml` and compiled Rust source no longer include SWC
    Ecma parser/codegen modules. `rewrite_script`,
    `rewrite_script_with_context`, and `rewrite_script_url` fail closed instead
    of producing browser-executable proxy/script code.
  - Rust HTML helper code no longer rewrites executable inline script/event
    handler bodies through AST/codegen; those surfaces are blocked or marked
    with fail-closed metadata.

## Phase 11 Legacy Service Worker Deletion Snapshot

- Deleted assets/routes:
  - `web/sw.js`, `web/sw-entry.mjs`, and `web/sw/*` are deleted.
  - `scripts/build.mjs` no longer emits a `sw.js` bundle or a
    `virtual:zeroproxy-sw-body` module.
  - `cmd/zeroproxy-server/main.go` has no Service Worker or worker-bootstrap
    route handler; `cmd/zeroproxy-server/routing_test.go` asserts legacy SW,
    `/__zp`, and worker-bootstrap spellings fail closed.
- Active path:
  - Host shell continues to open existing encrypted
    `/zp/p/<encrypted>#k=<key>` share routes.
  - `web/runtime/webapi/core.mjs` keeps `navigator.serviceWorker` as an
    unsupported virtual facade with no controller/registrations.
  - `test/js/membrane-invariants.test.js`, `test/js/static-policy.test.js`, and
    `test/js/compat-pipeline.test.js` assert the deleted assets and `/zp/api/*`
    source references stay absent from active runtime code.

This restores DOM-visible link semantics such as `a[href]` without leaking target URLs into renderer attributes, and clicks still route through the existing host `openTarget` path. The corpus harness also now treats a timed-out ZeroProxy navigation wait as recovered when the observed page already has a ready virtual document/render root, preventing late-heavy sites such as GitHub from being misclassified as navigation failures after successful rendering. Verification after refreshing the release artifact: `npm run build:web`, `npm run test:e2e`, `npm run test:corpus -- --sites naver-desktop --timeout-ms 60000`, `npm run test:corpus -- --sites github --timeout-ms 60000`, `npm run test:corpus -- --release-out test/e2e/representative-sites.release.json`, `npx biome format --write test/e2e/representative-sites.release.json`, and `npm run lint:js` ran after the change; the phase5 e2e asserts the rendered anchor has an `about:blank#zp-nav-*` href. That refresh reported 7/14 pass, 4 accepted expected deltas, and 3 triage sites before the later fixture-origin correction below.
  - follow-up: 2026-06-09, corrected the representative-site checked-in fixture origin from `127.0.0.1` to `localhost` so the GoNetworkBackend internal SOCKS path can fetch fixture HTML instead of rendering a synthetic `TARGET_CONNECT_FAILED` document. The embedded Google Maps fixture now loads the actual local HTML through the membrane, preserves the `iframe[data-corpus-google-maps]` selector, and verifies the virtual iframe as visible/non-blank/Google Maps-classified. The stale embedded-map expected navigation delta was removed from `test/e2e/representative-sites.json`; the refreshed release artifact now reports 8/14 pass, 4 expected deltas, and 2 triage sites (`naver-desktop`, `nytimes`). This restores Naver's skip-link anchors as DOM-visible `a[href]` elements without exposing raw target URLs. Verification: `go test ./internal/htmlsanitize`, focused `npm run test:corpus -- --sites naver-desktop --timeout-ms 60000`, full `npm run test:corpus -- --release-out test/e2e/representative-sites.release.json`, `node --test test/js/compat-corpus.test.js test/js/expected-deltas.test.js`, `go test ./internal/htmlsanitize ./cmd/wasm-kernel`, `npm run test:wasm`, and `npm run lint:js` passed after formatting.   - follow-up: 2026-06-09, native rendering now buffers dynamic DOM attributes/text emitted before a node has been attached to the rendered tree and flushes them when the corresponding `dom.appendChild` creates the native node. This fixes Google Maps' dynamically-built search/input/application shell: pre-append `aria-label`, `role`, and `id` mutations are no longer dropped, the `google-maps` representative site has no missing visible selectors, and the stale Maps expected rendering delta was removed. The corpus classifier no longer applies a 64-character text floor to genuinely sparse native pages, so low-text shells are judged by required selectors instead of failing solely because both native and ZeroProxy render little text. Verification: focused `npm run test:corpus -- --sites google-maps --timeout-ms 60000`, full `npm run test:corpus -- --release-out test/e2e/representative-sites.release.json`, `node --test test/js/virtual-dom.test.js test/js/compat-corpus.test.js test/js/expected-deltas.test.js`, `npm run build:web`, `npm run test:e2e`, `go test ./...`, `npm run test:wasm`, and `npm run lint:js` passed.   - follow-up: 2026-06-09, tightened the representative-site release classifier so only positive ZeroProxy request-failure deltas fail the gate; native-only aborts and benign post-document blob aborts no longer create false network triage, and script fail-close counters now use the same positive-delta rule. The text-ratio fallback also now requires a visible non-`body` native selector, avoiding false rendering failures for sparse or anti-automation native shells where the native page itself exposes no stable primary UI. Stale expected-delta annotations for Naver mobile, Google Search, GitHub, NYTimes, and Cloudflare were removed after the full live corpus proved them green; Amazon still keeps a source-level expected rendering annotation because it intermittently serves a sync-XHR anti-automation shell, but the latest full run rendered it without an actual expected delta. ## Update Rule

Every implementation change for the QuickJS/GoNetworkBackend migration must
update this file in the same branch when it changes behavior, status, scope,
verification, or known gaps. Keep entries concise and repository-backed.
